# 🟣 K.I.N.D — MASTER (strategy · decisions · history · session log)

> **🧭 Four-doc contract:** **KIND-MASTER** *(this doc)* = strategy, decisions, why, dated session log · **LAUNCH-PAD** = daily execution · **PRODUCT-INVENTORY** = status (one dot, one owner) · **V2-TRACKER** = future. **Conflict rule:** strategy/decision truth = here; status = PRODUCT-INVENTORY; daily action = LAUNCH-PAD; future = V2-TRACKER. No fifth core doc. *(This supersedes every older "two docs" / "three source docs" line below — those are archived history.)* **Money model of record → [`CASHFLOW-LAB.html`](./CASHFLOW-LAB.html)** (founder-locked 25 Jul, #556).

## ▶️ START HERE — the current state of the company *(26 Jul 2026 — supersedes every dated block below)*

**Locked by the founder, 26 Jul, in his words:** *"on 31 aug if this is not all done i stop."* That is the outside edge. Between now and then there is **one thing that decides whether we have a business: the product must be able to send from the client's own mailbox.** Everything else is already built or does not matter yet.

- **What we sell.** A **managed cold-outreach service** trading as **Milla&Vida** on the FIGSY engine. **Vida** = our operator console. **Milla** = the client portal (masked leads · 👍 approve / ✕ pass · concierge chat · meetings · reports). **Nexus** = each client's private brain. K.I.N.D remains the registered company.
- **The money, as it actually runs** (locked 24–25 Jul, #492/#541): one dollar wallet per client · first purchase **$99 = the onboarding pack, 100 approved leads included** · then a flat **$4 per approved lead, FINAL** · no expiry, no hold, no capture-on-booking, no release · a dead email is never charged · meetings are reported, not refunded · **only the client's 👍 ever spends — operators never**. Two gates protect it: **minimum 20 approvals** the first time round, and **30 days with no approvals suspends them** (they un-suspend on their own next approval, no operator in the loop).
- **The cost floor is ~$457/month, not ~$190** (#556 — the old figure omitted Smartlead ~$94, Instantly ~$37, Zoho ~$3 and the Anthropic runtime). Two consequences we now sell against: a client must approve **~13/month just to pay for their own inbox**, and the **$99 pack is −$52 in month one**. **The repeat is the business; the pack is the door.**
- **🛑 THE BLOCKER, stated plainly: we cannot deliver a paying client.** `figsy.ts:26` is `const FROM = COLD_FROM` — one shared module constant for every client — `client_inboxes` is read by **no send path**, and **no SMTP client is installed**. So *"sends on your own warmed inbox"* is not built. That is **#211**, 🔴 since 30 Jun, now broken into **#547–#553** (LAUNCH-PAD Block A). Founder-locked 26 Jul: **Instantly is OURS, Smartlead is the CLIENTS'**, and both run **inside our own product** — *"we use our own product for us"*, no CSV hand-off.
- **What we CAN do today: demo.** MBF (#544) is one fixed demo environment, always — 40 invented people, no migration needed, and it physically cannot send. **The demo is sellable; delivery is not.** So the honest sales position is: sell the demo, take the deposit, deliver when Block A lands.
- **Why the docs were rewritten today.** Founder: *"the one source of truth is so wrong."* He was right. `BUILD-STATUS.md` — a fifth status doc, forbidden by `CLAUDE.md` — claimed *"the ONLY items not built: #515 + CI"* while the whole sending spine was 🔴. It is retired (#555). LAUNCH-PAD is now one table of items, owners and mirrored dots, ordered Blocks A→E, with everything that does not get us live moved out and its new home named.
- **The rule that comes out of this:** every claim in a doc is either **read from the code in the same session** or it is marked as a claim. The last three sessions' worth of "done" that turned out to be half-done all trace to the same habit — reporting memory as evidence.

## 🏆 CREDIBILITY RULE — nothing public goes live half-full (locked 13 Jun)
> **Founder principle, applies to EVERYTHING public-facing:** we never ship a surface that looks empty or half-built — an empty Watch page, a Drop with no video, social icons linking to bare profiles, a thin Prompt Library. **Half-full = "not a real company" = lost credibility.** So: **build it fully on the branch now, but flip it LIVE only when it's genuinely populated.** Build ≠ go-live. Every website item below is gated this way (orphan/unlinked until full). Same for socials (164): icons built, hidden until the profiles have real content.

## 🎬 WEBSITE RESOURCES — CONTENT-FIRST PLAN (locked 13 Jun)
> **The principle:** Claude builds every page, all the copy, and the *video slots* **now**. The founder records video **after** and pastes it in — adding a video is a **one-line paste of a YouTube ID**, never a code change. Get the content shells **built**; flip them **live only when full** (per the Credibility Rule above).

**🔑 HOW THE DROP WORKS (founder, 13 Jun — corrected):** The Drop is a **SERIES**, not one post. **Each drop showcases 3–5 products/features.** We have a LOT of product → that's **many drops**. Build them **now, WITHOUT video** (drops are content showcases, complete without video). The **Prompt Library is the one big multi-item gallery**. **Product Video / Watch page → HOLD, do not build yet.**

**The resources — BUILD vs HOLD (13 Jun):**
| # | Resource | Decision | What it is | Inventory |
|---|----------|----------|------------|-----------|
| 1 | **The Drop** | 🛠️ **BUILD NOW (no video)** | A **series** of drop posts, each showcasing **3–5 products**. ## 🗓️ THE 3-MONTH PLAN (Jun → Sep) — where we're headed after launch
> The founder's-eye view of the next quarter. Item-level detail: `PRODUCT-INVENTORY.md` §3 + `V2-TRACKER.md`. Each phase is **gated** — don't start the next before its trigger.

### 📅 MONTH 1 — LAUNCH + CLEAR THE QUEUE (Fri 19 Jun → ~19 Jul)
**Goal: launch clean, onboard the first pilots, and ship the whole post-19 queue fast (velocity = the moat).**
- **Launch & GTM:** go live 19th · 10 warm outreach + LinkedIn 1/day · record real product demo + Drop 01 video + onboarding Looms · homepage proof block with real data · GTM funnel instrumentation.
- **Ship the locked design queue:** Alta-style **inbox rebuild** (112) · **agent "AI Family"** cards (125) · **sequence-builder** recolor (82) · **agent side-panel consistency** (113a) · the 25 R-release PRs · shell redesign.
- **Money & admin cleanup:** ⭐ **client invoicing** (136a — 🔒 locked 13 Jun: **USD billing · NO VAT until a financial benchmark · Stripe issues the receipt, we only pull & display it** in-portal) · **Partner Hub earnings ZAR → USD** · Flutterwave/2nd-processor decision.
- **Company engine hardening:** invite-email delivery · owner drill-down · manager role · per-rep routing/offboarding/calendars.
- **Onboarding & segmentation (174–177):** website→firmographics read · seat-based routing (1=self-serve / 2+=concierge) · 14-day company trial on bundled data · white-glove implementation (CRM + optional BYO-Apollo). Full plan: cashflow §13/§14.
- **First clients:** the multi-rep client + partner pipeline onboard; pilot feedback drives the next sprint.

### 📅 MONTH 2 — INTELLIGENCE LAYER (late Jul → Aug · GATED 10+ paying clients)
**Goal: turn the outcome data into a compounding advantage. Trigger: ~10+ paying clients producing real outcomes.**
- **The Learning Engine** (build order: Train-FIGSY RAG → evals → outcome feedback loop → contextual bandit → recall/memory → model routing). The African cross-client outcome data is the input no competitor can replicate.
- Intent signals · A/B → contextual bandit · morning-brief · ICP auto-refinement · conditional branching · waterfall enrichment live · adaptive send volume · Milla CRM pull.
- **Context-backed MCP server (#59)** · Product Hunt + G2 listings · ★ shared winning-play library (network-compounding).

### 📅 MONTH 3 — THE AGENT FAMILY + PRICING (Aug → Sep · GATED on margin data)
**Goal: expand the family and move toward outcome pricing. Trigger: proven unit margin ≥ ~28%.**
- **DENISE deep build (#54)** — auto-book · notetaker · objection handling · proposal-from-transcript · Vapi voice (she owns the phone).
- **LENA** (Customer Success agent) · **TONY** (Operations agent) · multi-agent orchestration.
- **Outcome pricing per meeting (#60)** (gated ≥28% margin) · built-in CRM Kanban · mobile app (PWA) · pan-African design partners.

**↔ Parallel/ongoing all quarter:** admin RLS refactor · deliverability monitoring · legal rings · key rotation · GTM content engine · per-country partner replication (Kenya + Ghana via partners).

- **🌍 AFRICA GTM — RESEARCH-HARDENED (10 Jun deep-research, ~40 sources; verdicts below are sourced, the tiering held):**
  - **Priority order confirmed: 1) SA · 2) Nigeria · 3) Kenya (fast-follow ~Q4'26) · 4) Ghana (opportunistic via NG partner) · 5) Egypt (defer indefinitely — Arabic-first, EF English "low" → FIGSY's English engine mismatched).** Evidence: SA = Africa's best English (EF #13 globally) + best card rails for recurring billing + most mature SaaS market; Nigeria = largest SMB pool + best-of-Africa Apollo coverage (with SA) + partner-led entry matches how Nigerian B2B actually buys; Kenya = #1 VC funding (~$934M '25) **but energy-skewed (weak sales-tool signal)**, thinner Apollo data, M-Pesa-first billing weak for card recurring.
  - **🟢 THE LANE IS OPEN:** **no African-built AI-SDR/cold-email competitor found**; global tools (Apollo/Instantly/Smartlead) have zero localized African GTM, pricing, payments, or POPIA/NDPR positioning. **Watch: Trembi** (East Africa — positioning as "Apollo alternative for Africa"). **Concrete wedge: Apollo itself doesn't accept African mobile-money/local payments — we do (Flutterwave).**
  - **Data ceiling CONFIRMED:** Apollo strongest (for Africa) in SA + Lagos enterprise/mid-market; **thin in Kenya/Ghana and for SMBs generally**; PDL fallback = breadth not depth. Proprietary African contact graph = the real moat (roadmap). Quantify empirically: the 4-country previewCount test.
  - **Nigeria billing note:** research rates **Paystack subscriptions** the best-fit for NG SaaS billing (bank-transfer/USSD culture, card declines high) — we have Flutterwave; evaluate Paystack as an NG-specific addition when the first NG client lands.
  - **Deliverability:** no evidence African-origin mail is penalised *when sent from reputable infra with SPF/DKIM/DMARC* (which we do); risk attaches to IP/domain reputation, not nationality. Action: seed-list placement tests into Gmail/Outlook ZA/NG/KE post-launch.
  - **Runway honesty (estimate):** at $29–99/seat, ~$10k MRR ≈ 100–300 seats ≈ 10–30 SMB clients ≈ plausibly **6–12 months post-launch** on trust-led African sales — **unless the partner channel produces multi-client batches → the partner motion is the highest-leverage GTM decision of the year** (pattern evidence: Flutterwave/Salesforce-Africa scaled via partners; Yoco shows direct works only where physically present → SA-direct + NG-via-Demmy is exactly right).
  - **🤝 PARTNER CHANNEL (the highest-leverage GTM lever) — partners on the books:** **#1 Demmy (Nigeria, live).** **#2 Ireland partner (added 16 Jun)** — small but **very well connected**; sells the **trade-playbook** motion (electricians · plumbers · HVAC · photographers · locksmiths). **Ireland is NOT a target market** — this is an *opportunistic, partner-led channel*, not a market entry. **Guardrails (founder, 16 Jun):** build **NOTHING** Ireland-specific · partner uses **BYO-Apollo / own data** (keeps the single-key §13 ToS exposure flat) · price **USD** · **GDPR** already covers the EU · **Africa stays the build priority** (don't let a well-connected "yes" bend the roadmap). Rationale: bounded downside (the partner does the selling), free upside (early developed-market revenue + case studies/logos + a proof the motion travels). **Sales asset: `docs/PARTNER-BRIEF.md`** (pitch · 5 trade playbooks · pricing · 20% recurring terms).
  - **🇺🇸 US VERDICT: US-LATER, CLEARLY (sourced):** ~110 AI-SDR vendors, $400M+ VC, median vendor revenue only ~$2.2M; 11x scandal (fake customers, ~$3M real vs ~$14M claimed ARR, "70–80% churn" per employee — TechCrunch Mar'25); US cold-email replies degraded to **~3.4% average ('26)**; Gmail/Yahoo 0.1% working spam ceiling; premature multi-market scaling = best-documented startup killer (Startup Genome ~70%). US arguments (USD revenue, data depth) are capturable WITHOUT US GTM: price in USD (we do), US-grade infra (we do). **Tripwires to revisit US (founder-gated):** (a) ~$10–20k MRR / 25+ paying clients across SA+NG, (b) >85% 6-month logo retention, (c) FIGSY African reply rates beating the 3.4% US average. Low-cost middle path then: serve African companies selling *into* the US.
  - *(Original directional tiering below, kept for the record:)*
  - **Tier 1 engines: 🇿🇦 South Africa** (best Apollo coverage — dogfood ICP proved ~72k · POPIA built = category-of-one compliance claim · English · cards work · home turf) **+ 🇳🇬 Nigeria** (largest SMB market · **partner Demmy already live** · Flutterwave solves payments · watch: thinner data, NDPR ⏸ until first NG client, sender-reputation care).
  - **Tier 2: 🇰🇪 Kenya** (East-Africa hub, fintech-adoption culture, ODPC ⏸ until first KE client) · **🇬🇭 Ghana** (easy English entry — partner-led, not HQ focus).
  - **Tier 3 (defer): 🇪🇬 Egypt / 🇲🇦 Morocco** (Arabic/French localisation we don't have) · 🇷🇼🇲🇺🇧🇼 (tiny volume, opportunistic).
  - **How we target:** ① dogfood — FIGSY sells K.I.N.D (warmup campaign IS this; scale it) · ② replicate the Demmy partner model per country (1 partner each KE+GH in month 1–2; trust-broker markets) · ③ lead with compliance as marketing (POPIA/NDPR = claims competitors can't make) · ④ open low per-seat pricing as the weapon vs $500+/mo US tools · ⑤ **before pushing any country, run `previewCount` on the canonical ICP — if Apollo returns thin, that market waits for the PDL/multi-source layer (now wired, key-gated).**

    - **Positioning line to steal verbatim: "level up your whole team to your top performer."**
    - **Two-audience framing:** the rep feels TIME saved; the **owner pays for VISIBILITY** (accurate pipeline/forecast without chasing reps) → sell to both; owner = budget holder = 2c command centre.
    - **Headline KPI/ROI: sell in TIME SAVED PER SEAT** (e.g. "~8h/rep/month") — surface it on the dashboard; it's the number the owner renews on.
    - **Meeting-Prep agent GAP:** nothing preps the human for the booked meeting (last-call summary + suggested questions) → clean **Denise #54** addition ("FIGSY opens, Denise preps you to close").
    - **Mobile-first** (#15 PWA) is a stronger play in Africa than for Glean.

### 🔍 HONEST STATUS — be critical (8 Jun, end of session)
> ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED — 8-Jun historical snapshot, kept for the record.** Most of this was resolved 8–10 Jun: the system **deployed + is live**, **T8 + T1 passed**, a **16-test vitest suite exists**, P-a/CAL-min portal fields shipped, migration 012 ran. For CURRENT status read the RESUME-HERE block + MASTER TIMELINE at the top. The hard truths below were true on 8 Jun.
>
> Cuts through the green ticks above. A lot of code shipped today; **almost none of it
> is deployed or verified in a real environment.** Read this before assuming we're "on track."

**The hard truths:**
1. **Nothing is deployed; nothing is end-to-end tested.** There is **no automated test suite** (no `test` script). All of D1–D5 (List-Unsubscribe, plain-text, cold-FROM), the warmup cap, P-a signer, and the admin fixes are **typecheck-clean only** — never run against a real DB, Resend, or inbox. **Typecheck ≠ working.** First real proof comes only at the Tue 9 deploy + Smoke Tests.
2. **P-a + CAL-min are backend-only → currently INERT.** No portal field exists to set `signer_name` / `booking_url`, so clients can't use them. "Done" = misleading; they're *backend-ready*, gated on UI + migration 012.
3. **Warmup cap has a blind spot.** It counts `figsy_sent_emails`, but the **day-1 batch path inserts NULL enrollment/campaign ids into NOT-NULL columns → those inserts fail → day-1 sends are never counted.** The cap reliably guards the *sequence* path only. (Pre-existing constraint bug — needs a fix or the cap to count from `outcome_events`.)
4. **`signer_name` is a latent landmine.** If the portal sends it before migration 012 runs, the `PATCH /me` upsert fails → **breaks ALL settings saves.** Migration 012 MUST run before any UI sends it.
5. **Admin still bypasses RLS.** The admin frontend uses the `service_role` key across ~13 pages — the biggest architectural security gap, **unfixed** (Week 1–2 refactor).
6. **Launch rests entirely on one un-rehearsed Tue 9 deploy** going perfectly: merge→main, run migrations 010 + 012, set/verify env vars (cold-FROM, admin key), nothing else breaks. No dry run yet.
7. **Smoke tests T1–T7 not started.** The real end-to-end verification hasn't begun. T1 (fresh signup) has never been run, ever.

**Fair risk read for Fri 19:** the *code* is in good shape and progressing fast; the **gap is verification + deploy choreography, not features.** Date is still plausible **only if** the Tue 9 deploy is clean AND Smoke Test 2 passes first try — neither is proven. Realistic buffer is thin: the day-1 cap fix, the admin RLS refactor, and any smoke-test failures all compete for the same 2–3 days. If the deploy slips or smoke tests find real breaks, **Mon 22 is the honest fallback.**

**Most valuable next moves (in order):** (1) do a **dry-run deploy to a staging/throwaway** to de-risk Tue 9; (2) **run Smoke Test T1 fresh-signup** ASAP — it's never been tested; (3) fix the day-1 cap blind spot; (4) build the P-a/CAL-min portal fields *with* migration 012 so they're actually usable.

### 🎯 V2 MONTH-END PLAN — LIVE BY 30 JUN (locked 8 Jun by founder)
> Founder set the target + chose the scope: **"Per-rep + company OS."** This is THE core that must be in production by 30 Jun. It is a *scoped* V2, not all 18 sections — forcing everything would build the half-baked system we refuse to ship.
>
> **🚦 GATE (day 1, non-negotiable):** V1 in prod is NOT verified yet — warmup/T8 deliverability never tested. We do **not** build per-rep on an unverified base. **9am: Step 5 (first FIGSY warmup campaign) + T8 (deliverability), then T1–T10.** Foundation must be green before staging build starts.
>
> **IN by 30 Jun (the durable money engine):**
> - **#88 per-rep** — each rep their own autonomous FIGSY (identity · calendar · booking · leads scoped to the member, not the workspace).
> - **Owner Command Centre** (V2-9) — one company dashboard over all reps.
> - **Seats** + **Usage & Budget** (request → approve/deny, hybrid seat+usage) — one company payment, N FIGSYs. *(Founder: "vital.")*
> - **Performance funnel** (§17) — per-rep + company rollup (per-rep is meaningless without the rollup).
>
> **SLIPS to July (designed, not month-end — layer ON the per-rep foundation):** full branching sequence builder (#89) · multi-channel LinkedIn/WhatsApp/SMS · Train-FIGSY (§18) · Integrations Hub (#84) · §16 Smart Inbox rich taxonomy · the V2 cosmetic redesign items (V2-1/2/3/4/6/8/10/11/12).
>
> **DATED CRITICAL PATH (22 days):**
> - **Wk0 · Mon 8 – Fri 12 — VERIFY + STABILISE.** 9am smoke gate (Step 5 + T8 → T1–T10). Warmup ramping. First client onboarded on shared model as pilot. Partner solid. **Finalise #88 schema design + stand up staging (hard prereq).**
> - **Wk1 · Mon 15 – Fri 19 — PER-REP FOUNDATION (staging).** Slice 1 per-rep identity (calendar/booking/signer/leads → `client_members`). Slice 2 per-rep ownership (campaigns/sent-emails owned + meeting routing to the right rep's calendar). Migrations written + dry-run.
> - **Wk2 · Mon 22 – Fri 26 — COMPANY OS (staging).** Slice 3 seat billing (per-seat wallets + company budget + request/approve usage). Slice 4 Owner Command Centre + Performance funnel rollup. End-to-end integrate on staging.
> - **Wk3 · Mon 29 – Tue 30 — HARDEN + SHIP.** Migration dry-run on a staging clone, full per-rep smoke (new T11 per-rep flow), deploy to prod **30 Jun**. Mon 29 is the buffer for slippage.
>
> **Risks:** (1) the smoke gate finds real deliverability/prod breakage → eats Wk0, pushes everything right; (2) the workspace→member migration on a live client's data is the single riskiest step — staging dry-run is mandatory; (3) scope creep back toward "everything" — hold the line, July layers the rest.

### 🔄 SESSION LOG (newest first — append one line per working session)
- **27 Jul (🔌 PROMPT 4 WIRED — the missing hand-off built, and the whole thing BLOCKED on Instantly's billing):** the founder caught that Prompt 4 shipped the Instantly client and the mapping and **wired neither into anything** — the grep for callers returned *tests only*, while the PR read as complete. That grep now returns **`approve-lead.ts:318`**. `lib/instantly-push.ts` sits at **step 9**, after the enrol, because a lead must be paid for, revealed and in a sequence before anyone sends on its behalf; it is wrapped so it **cannot fail an approve the client has already been charged for**, and re-checks every gate itself rather than trusting the caller. **The house client is `HOUSE_CLIENT_ID`, an env var — not a name match**, because exact-string matching on a company name has bitten us twice (`MBF Holdings` vs the live `MBF Demo`, #584/#582): a name is a label a human edits, an id is not. **Unset means nothing is ever pushed** — fail closed, since guessing wrong means emailing real people from the wrong account. **Four refusals are deliberately silent** (demo · kill-switch · a client, who goes via Smartlead · no key) because alerting on the system working correctly would page the founder on **every approval** and train them to ignore it. **🛑 BLOCKED, not broken:** the founder created the key with the right four scopes and `GET /api/v2/accounts` returned **HTTP 402 Payment Required** — the plan/billing limit written into `NOT_POSSIBLE[]` on 26 Jul, now **confirmed rather than predicted**. Nothing is live-verified against Instantly and cannot be until the bill/plan is settled; founder-decided: *"wait for the bill to be paid, won't do this until the rest of the system is good."* Reply pulling and rate-limit backoff remain NOT-POSSIBLE. 630 across the suite.
- **27 Jul (🎨 P3-1 — EVERY ERROR IN THE VIDA COCKPIT RENDERED AS A SUCCESS: fifteen, not one):** the founder flagged the Run/Pause refusal; reading every setter and every render site under P10 showed it was **systemic**. One `saveMsg` string, **ten render sites all hardcoded `text-[#0e7c86]`** — teal — and **fifteen failure paths** writing into it: *"Could not save the ICP"*, *"Could not send the ask"*, *"Failed to load people"*, *"Could not update the booking"*, and the refusal added by #590. **An operator scans a console by colour before words**, which is exactly how fifteen failures sat unnoticed — on the screen whose whole prompt was about not lying, found while fixing that screen. **Fixed by making tone impossible to omit** rather than recolouring one line: `saveMsg` is a `{ text, tone }` pair, so a caller **cannot write a failure without saying it is one**, and the class comes from the tone instead of from whatever was copied into the nearest JSX. **The regression guard is the point:** unit tests prove the helper is right but can never prove the ten sites USE it — and that gap *was* the defect, because a correct colour was always available and the sites hardcoded one anyway. A test now **reads the page source** and fails on any teal `saveMsg` render, any raw `{saveMsg}` (which would print `[object Object]` at an operator), and any bare `setSaveMsg('Could not…')`; the admin app has no test runner, so it runs from the API suite. Proven red twice — collapsing the helper, and reverting **one** render site, which the guard names by line. Zero new type errors (11 pre-existing before and after). 591 across the suite. Minted #597.
- **27 Jul (📭 P2-2 — THE ALERT BLAMED THE PROSPECT WHEN THE FAILURE WAS OURS):** found by reading `figsy.ts` 198–240 end to end under P10. R3 already alerted — that half was built — but **the reason stayed in `console.error`**, and the alert said *"the body arrived empty"* for **four different situations**, describing three of them **wrongly**: the fetch returned **HTTP 500**; the fetch **threw**; **`RESEND_API_KEY` was unset so the fetch never ran at all** — and the alert still claimed it *"returned nothing"*; and the single case the wording actually fitted. **The distinction is the whole point:** an empty email is a prospect quirk to ignore, while HTTP 500 is **our pipeline down and every reply being lost** — told the wrong one, the founder ignores an outage. The route now carries the real reason out of the fetch block instead of logging it away (status **and** body text, or the thrown message verbatim), and `describeBodyFetch()` — pure and tested — turns it into a sentence naming what happened and **whose fault it is**. The missing-key reading is new, and is the one the old wording lied about hardest. **6 tests, all proven red** against the old single message, including one asserting the readings are **all different** — the fix stated as an assertion — and one that it **never blames the prospect when the failure was ours**. 582 across the suite. Minted #596.
- **27 Jul (🔁 P2-1 — THE AI CLASSIFIER WAS INSIDE THE PER-CLIENT LOOP, and I put it there fixing R1):** found by reading `figsy.ts` 127–505 **end to end** under the new P10, after the founder's diagnosis — *"you grep everything off last action and never read end to end"*. R1 wrapped the reply handler in a per-client loop so a prospect worked by two clients lands in **both** threads; `classifyReply` — an **Anthropic call taking only the body**, nothing client-specific — was inside it. Two costs per shared reply, and worse: **the model is not deterministic**, so the same email could come back **`hot` for one client and `warm` for the other** — one getting the founder alert, the web push and the CRM deal, the other getting none of it, **for the same email**. Hoisted above the loop. **Proven by the codebase's third route-level test**, because a unit test could never have seen it: `classifyReply` is correct, `findLeadMatches` is correct, and the reply spine already had 21 passing tests — **the bug was in the SHAPE of the route**, which is precisely what grep cannot show. Six tests drive the real Express handler: classify once for two clients · both threads still written (R1 not undone) · both rows carry the same classification · and an unmatched reply never reaches the classifier, so a stranger's email costs nothing. Red proven by putting the call back inside the loop → `expected 2 to be 1`. 576 across the suite. Minted #595.
- **26 Jul (📋 THE CLAUSE TABLE — the discipline made a required ARTIFACT rather than a promise):** founder, after catching Prompt 4 reported complete while nothing called it: *"your big issue is you grep everything off last action and never read end to end"*, then *"how do i now get you to do the same action on every new prompt"*. **P10 (read the path end to end) and P11 (every prompt answered with a clause table)** added to `docs/PRODUCT-RULES.md`, plus **§5a** carrying the exact wording the founder pastes **before** each work prompt — so it binds a fresh session with no memory of today. **Why an artifact, not a behaviour:** a behaviour is forgotten. The clause-by-clause read was committed to after Prompt 1 and **dropped by Prompt 4**. An artifact is enforceable without trust — a missing table means the PR comes back unread, exactly as the pasted-red rule already works. Three things the wording pins, each earned: the table is built **BEFORE** the work (so a clause like *"on approved leads, push the lead"* sits visible as a row from the start); it is **quoted, never paraphrased** (paraphrasing is how *"every /operator endpoint"* became *"the System screen"*); and **any ❌ goes in the first line and the PR title** (Prompt 4's NOT-POSSIBLE items sat near the bottom while the report led with what worked). **Every miss this week was invisible to grep** — a module nothing imported, a generator writing to a scratchpad that dies with the container, a green test *requiring* the value the database rejects, a refusal rendered in the success colour. Grep only ever returns what you already suspected. Minted #594.
- **26 Jul (🛑 THE GATE WAS GREEN FOR A REASON THAT DID NOT REPRODUCE — and the gate itself caught it):** the founder ran `ship.sh` and got `TypeError: loadError is not a function`, 21 tests failed, **NOTHING WAS DEPLOYED**. **The gate did exactly its job** — it refused to ship a red build to a live site. What it caught was **mine**. #590/#591 moved pure logic into `@kind/shared` so both consoles could share one copy; that package declares `"main": "./dist/index.js"` and **`packages/shared/dist` is gitignored** — a build artifact, not source — and `check.sh` never built it. So every check resolved `@kind/shared` to *whatever that machine last built*: fresh in the container it was authored in, stale on the founder's Mac. **The same commit was green in one place and 21-red in the other.** Fifth instance of the family: bash 3.2 (#578), BSD awk (#579), a hardcoded `/home/user/KIND` and a manifest the generator never wrote (#582) — every one tooling that works where it was written and nowhere else, and every one invisible from the container. **Fixed in both halves**, because either alone leaves a hole: `apps/api/vitest.config.ts` aliases `@kind/shared` to its **source**, so a unit test can never again depend on whether somebody ran a build; and **`check.sh` builds the package as step 1**, because the type-check and both Next builds legitimately consume `dist`. **Proven cold:** deleted the dist entirely → without the alias the founder's failure reproduces exactly → with it, **570 tests pass on an absent dist** → and the full gate from that same cold state is **6/6 green**. Minted #592.
- **26 Jul (💜 PROMPT 3, B of B — THE MILLA SCREENS THAT TOLD A PAYING CLIENT LESS THAN THE TRUTH):** split from #590 by **audience, not ticket** — this half is what a client sees, so the preview-first rule applies. **#563 — the billing page had no concept of the pack.** It read `/credits` and knew **only the wallet**, so a client who had just paid **$99** saw a **$0 balance and no mention of the 100 approvals they had bought**, on the one screen that exists to explain what they paid for. The cause is structural: the pack is a **counted quota, not a wallet credit** (#541) — the money sits in the *entitlement*, not the *balance* — so a wallet-only page could never show it. Now reads `pack` from `milla-summary`, the same `packState` the desk and greeting use, and a **failed** pack fetch says *"this does not mean you have none"* rather than rendering the wallet-only lie in a new hat. **#570, four desk gaps:** `Promise.all` **blanked the entire dashboard** when either endpoint failed, including the half that had loaded (the billing page already used `allSettled`; the desk did not) · **`pass()` never refreshed**, so the KPI still read *"3 leads awaiting"* after all three were passed, and with a 50-row cap passing one never pulled the next in · **the KPI counts every lead awaiting while the list is capped at 50**, so 120 waiting rendered as *"120 leads awaiting you"* above 50 rows with nothing explaining the other 70 — which reads as us having lost them · and **the 402 invented its figure in the browser** (`ids.length * 4`, blind to both the wallet balance and the leads still inside the pack), now using the server's numbers when sent and otherwise **stating the rule rather than a total we cannot stand behind** — a wrong number on a payment screen costs more trust than no number. **13 tests, all proven RED first**: the old arithmetic printed **$80** where the server had said **$34.50**. 570 across the suite. Minted #591 as **🟣**, not 🟡 — client-facing, so it wants eyes on preview before live. Gates: api tsc · full vitest · both consoles build · doc-lint — green.
- **26 Jul (🖥 PROMPT 3, A of B — THE VIDA CONSOLE LIED TWICE):** honesty fixes only, founder-authorised. **#564 — the audit log recorded `pause_campaign` for everything.** Pressing **Run it** wrote *"paused the campaign"*; so did a rename or a cap change. The audit log is the only record of who did what to a client's account, and on the most consequential action it said **the opposite of what happened** — worse than no log, because an empty log makes you go and look while a confidently wrong one makes you stop. Two new actions and a pure, tested `campaignAuditAction()` derived from what the patch actually does. **Second half: the refusal was never shown** — `setCampaignStatus` parsed the response and threw it away, so a refusing API produced a button that appeared to do nothing, while the comment claimed the failure was *"surfaced by the reload"*. Same class as the Delete button that deleted nothing. **#565 — a failed load rendered as an empty board.** All three console loads were `.catch(() => {})`, so a downed endpoint left state empty and Vida rendered a calm **"nothing to do"** on the one screen whose purpose is telling the operator what to work on next; the worklist header sat on **"Loading…" forever**, which reads as *still working* rather than *broken*. `panelView()` keeps loading · empty · failed apart and marks a failed load **untrustworthy even though its count is also zero** — the assertion that is the entire bug. Placed in `@kind/shared`, the one package both consoles can import, because the admin app has no test runner and a second copy is how the two would drift. **13 tests, both proven RED first.** Verified zero new type errors: admin `tsc` reports 11 pre-existing on unmodified main and 11 with these changes. 557 across the suite. **Split from the Milla half deliberately** — #563/#570 are client-facing and fall under the preview-first rule. Minted #590. Gates: api tsc · full vitest · both consoles build · doc-lint — green.
- **26 Jul (📬 PROMPT 2 — THE REPLY CHAIN: five defects fixed once, in the spine every provider will flow through):** founder-authorised, *"build them provider-agnostic"*. Resend delivers replies today; Instantly and Smartlead deliver them by API next under #577 — so the matching and opt-out logic moved into `lib/reply-ingest.ts` instead of staying inside the Resend webhook, where all three providers would have grown their own copy of the same five bugs. **R1, the worst: a reply matching leads at TWO clients was dropped forever.** `.eq('email', x).maybeSingle()` **errors** on more than one row, so `lead` came back null and the handler returned a silent 200 — and two clients prospecting the same person is ordinary, because the pool is shared. Every match is returned now and the reply routed into **each** client's thread; picking one would hand one client's reply to another, which is worse than dropping it. A failed lookup **throws** rather than returning empty, because "the query broke" and "nobody matched" must never look the same. **R2/R3:** an empty body or a failed Resend body-fetch both ended in the same silent 200 — Resend's `email.received` is metadata-only, so those two are indistinguishable. Both now alert **naming the provider message id**, so the reply can be opened in the provider and answered by hand. Nothing was ever lost; only the knowledge that it happened. **R6:** a hot reply sets the enrollment to `replied`, and the lookup filtered to `['enrolled','in_progress']` — so the follow-up *"actually, yes, let's talk"* skipped **every** hot path. Fixed, with a test pinning that the lookup and active-sending sets never collapse into one another. **R7:** the three opt-out writes were unchecked, and the blocklist is the single suppression source the send path reads — a silent failure means **we keep emailing someone who told us to stop**, the one failure here with a legal edge. All three checked, every failure collected, the alert **names the address**, and the blocklist is written first because the rest is bookkeeping. **21 tests, every defect proven RED against the old behaviour first.** 544 across the suite. Minted #589. Gates: api tsc · full vitest · both consoles build · doc-lint — green.
- **26 Jul (🧹 EVERY INTEGRITY CHECK NOW IGNORES OUR OWN INVENTED DATA — one shared rule, not six patches):** founder: *"yes i want perfect. wont settle for less"*. **Seven of the eight checks scanned every row in the database, counting demo accounts as real clients.** Nothing was wrong on the live run — all eight came back clean — but it is the class that produced **three false answers on the first run**, the worst a **CRITICAL** *"a client has paid and cannot be delivered"* about an account that **cannot send by design**. **The risk was never the wrong number; it is that the founder stops trusting the screen** — a check that cries wolf about invented companies is one you learn to ignore, and then it misses the real one. Fixed as **one rule**: the demo ids are read **once** per report and handed to every check, so eight checks can never disagree about which accounts are demos. `packAndWallet` had been running its **own** `is_demo` query — a second source of truth for the same fact — and now uses the shared set; exactly **one** `is_demo` lookup remains in the file. The judgement is pure and tested, including the two cases that decide whether it is safe: **no demos at all drops nothing**, and **all-demo yields clean rather than hidden**. `doubleCharged` had to start selecting `client_id`, because it reads only `reference` and so had **no way to tell a demo's charge from a real one**. **If the demo lookup itself fails the ENTIRE report renders UNANSWERED** — a green result would be a lie in both directions. **One check is deliberately exempt and says so in the code:** `ledgerTypesLive` asks whether the live CHECK constraint allows the wallet types, and a row proves it accepted them **whoever wrote it** — filtering would discard valid evidence. 26 → **32** tests in that file, **505** across the suite. Minted #588. Gates: api tsc · full vitest · both consoles build · doc-lint — green.
- **26 Jul (🎭 THE DEMO WROTE VALUES THE PRODUCT NEVER WRITES — and a green test was enforcing one of them):** the rebuild after #1178 returned `MBF built with 2 problem(s)` — `figsy_enrollments_status_check` and `figsy_replies_classification_check`. **Not a schema gap this time: the demo seed had invented its own vocabulary.** Enrolments were written `status: 'active'` while **the product writes `'enrolled'`** (`figsy.ts:1609`); and one reply was classified **`'objection'`** — a value **nothing else in the system uses**: the classifier never emits it, the live CHECK constraint rejects it, and the unibox cannot render it. It existed in exactly one place, the demo. **And `demo-mbf.test.ts` asserted `expect(kinds).toContain('objection')`** — a passing test *requiring* the value the database rejects, which is why this reached a live rebuild with a green suite behind it. **A test that pins invented data is worse than no test: it makes the wrong thing load-bearing.** Both values now match what the product writes; the objection story is untouched because it lives in the reply body, where it always did. New guard test pins every MBF classification to the union of the live constraint and what the unibox renders — **proven red on `'objection'`, green when restored**. **The principle, stated once:** a demo that writes values the real path never writes is not a demo of this product — it is a second, divergent implementation that happens to look similar, and it will keep breaking against constraints the real path satisfies. Minted #587. Gates: api tsc · full vitest (24 in this file) · both consoles build · doc-lint — green.
- **26 Jul (📊 THE SYSTEM SCREEN REPORTED OUR OWN TEST DATA AS THE BUSINESS — fix 8 of 8 from the baseline):** the founder read the first baseline and caught it: the screen said the kill-switch was **OFF**, and four sections later said **"95 sent · 29 replies"**. Both rendered by the same report; only one could be true. Every one of those rows was **seeded** — `demo-mbf.ts` and `seed-company.ts` write straight into `figsy_sent_emails` so a demo doesn't show 0 replies in front of a prospect — and the probe counted the whole table. **Same defect as #543**, where demos polluted Vida's headline numbers: our own invented data handed back to us as if it were revenue. **The dangerous half was the alarm, not the vanity number.** That row turns BROKEN above a 3% bounce rate — *"you are burning the sending domain"* — and it was computing that rate over **invented `.invalid` addresses**, so the single number meant to protect the sending domain was derived from people who do not exist. Demos are excluded via their campaigns now (`figsy_sent_emails` has no `client_id`, only `campaign_id`), with an **empty-`in` guard** so a system with no demos cannot throw and render NOT-MEASURED on a healthy setup. The demo count is still **shown** rather than dropped, because those rows do exist. **Flagged and NOT fixed here, for the founder's call:** six integrity checks still scan every row without excluding demos — all six returned clean live, so nothing is wrong today, but it is the same class that produced three false answers on the first run. Minted #586. Gates: api tsc · full vitest · both consoles build · doc-lint — green.

- **26 Jul (🔁 PROMPT 1 FINISHED AGAINST ITS OWN SPEC — the missing endpoint probe, three wrong answers, and a FOURTH check that was passing without running):** founder: *"is everything in that prompt right… because you claim stuff but it is never done"*, then *"d3 gap and d4 wrong. no i want everything in step 1 done right."* So I re-read the authorising prompt line by line instead of from memory. **The gap:** it asked for *"every `/operator` endpoint answering vs erroring"* and the System screen never called one — it probed dependencies, schema, clients and Vida state and skipped the routes every Vida screen is actually made of. `operatorEndpoints()` now calls **all 20 read-only operator GETs** over real HTTP against the running process; **5xx or no answer = BROKEN**, because a route that errors renders a Vida screen empty and *empty looks identical to "nothing to do"*. **The three wrong answers**, all from the first LIVE run and all the same shape — judgement inlined in a query where no test could reach it: the **demo** was reported CRITICAL *"paid but cannot send"* when a demo cannot send **by design**; the **double-grant** check matched a wallet balance alone, flagging a client credited by `manual_grant` who never paid us; and the **MBF demo** was matched on the exact string `MBF Holdings` while the live account is `MBF Demo`, so it reported *"does not exist"* about an account sitting in the client list — right conclusion, false reason, which is not a report. All three are pure predicates now with 9 tests (17 → **26**). **Then the fourth instance found itself,** because I watched `update-board.sh` print instead of trusting its exit code: #578's own fix — six named counters replacing `declare -A` — **left three `${count["🟢"]}` reads inside `--check`**, where bash evaluates the subscript as arithmetic, so it died with `🟢: syntax error`, **abandoned the if-block mid-way**, fell through to the plain-print line and **exited 0**. Fixed at the root: **falling out of `--check` is now itself exit 2**, and doc-lint separates *"the check could not run"* from *"the doc drifted"*. - **26 Jul (📮 THE SHARED `FROM` IS GONE — option B locked, and the product can finally speak to a mailbox that isn't ours):** founder pushed back on my recommendation and was right to. I had said *"A, because deliverability is reputation and theirs is warm"* — **which is not an argument for A at all**: warming is a property of the MAILBOX, not of who presses send. Provider warms it either way. Once that fell away the real trade-off was visible, and it is the founder's own point: **we have already built the sending brain** — sequence engine, send window, daily caps, A/B subjects, kill-switch, reply classifier — and **A puts a second brain in charge of *when* an email goes out**, which you cannot have. **B locked.** B also earns what A never could: a client who says *"use my own Google Workspace"* is a client A has to turn away. Founder then sent the Zapmail/SmartSenders screen as proof — **$45 = domain $18/yr + 3 mailboxes at $9/mo, on real Google and Outlook**, not a black-box sender, which is what makes B buildable at all. **Built:** `lib/sending-inbox.ts` — the sender is resolved per client from `client_inboxes`, as a **pure decision function** (18 tests) so the ordering rules and every refusal path are provable without a database. Two rules it pins, both money: a **`warming` branded mailbox never sends** (sending on it is exactly what un-warms it), so during the SOP's pooled→branded overlap the **pooled** one sends; and a half-configured preferred mailbox is **skipped, not fatal**, so a blank branded row can't take a paying client off the air. `lib/mailer.ts` — nodemailer, one transport per send (a pooled connection shared across clients could put one client's mail down another's authenticated session), returning the **same verdict shape as `interpretSend`** so every #338 rollback path — delete the phantom row, roll the claim back, alert, leave it due — keeps working byte-for-byte. `lib/inbox-secret.ts` — AES-256-GCM, fresh IV per encryption, authenticated so a tampered value **throws** rather than handing garbage to a mail server and locking out a warmed mailbox; `describeCipher` is the only thing any surface may show (25 tests). **`figsy.ts:26` — the line this whole blocker was named after — is deleted**, and `COLD_FROM` is no longer imported by the cold path, so there is no fallback left to fall back TO. **Retired the #311 `RESEND_API_KEY` guard on both cold paths**: cold mail no longer touches Resend, so gating it on that key would have blocked every send on a credential the path stopped using — the concern behind #311 now sits one level lower, in a resolve that refuses loudly instead of writing a phantom row. **🧍 Owed: `INBOX_SECRET_KEY` (`openssl rand -hex 32`) and one mailbox's SMTP details.** ⚠️ Flagged: Google and Microsoft both block plain SMTP by default (App Password / SMTP AUTH per mailbox). Gates: api tsc clean · **466** vitest · doc-lint green. Migration `20260726_inbox_smtp` (additive, idempotent) is in the Engine card.

Founder locked two gates. **① Minimum 20, hard.** `lib/approval-batch.ts` (18 tests) + a server-side `batchGate` on **every** door into the money path — `/approve`, `/reveal` and the new `/approve-batch` — because a disabled button is a suggestion anyone can step around with a fetch. So the "password authentication failed" was never a wrong password — it was my parser cutting a correct one in half. Gates: api tsc + **310** vitest · portal + admin build · doc-lint.
- **25 Jul (🔑 IPv6 SOLVED, now it's the password — and GitHub has removed the Supabase OAuth app entirely):** the pooler fallback worked: *"Tried 19 address(es); last error: **password authentication failed for user postgres**"*. That is a completely different failure from ENETUNREACH — **the database answered**, so the IPv4 route is fixed and this is now a credentials problem. Three things came out of it. ① **Supavisor reports the user as `postgres` even when we connect as `postgres.<ref>`** — it splits the dot into user + tenant — so that name in the error is expected and not evidence of a malformed username; the error text now says so, because it reads like the bug and isn't. ② **My own message was misleading:** it reported *"Tried 19 address(es)"* when the loop had stopped after two (it breaks on a non-routing error, correctly) — it printed the number of candidates GENERATED, not attempted. Fixed, and auth failures now get their own message that says plainly *"the network route is fine, this is a password problem"*, because conflating the two is what sent us chasing IPv6 twice. ③ **`passwordOf()` was truncating at the first `@`** — a generated password containing an unencoded `@` (invalid but very common in pasted connection strings) would come back short, and a short password reads back as *"password authentication failed"*: a parsing bug wearing a credentials bug's clothes. Now greedy to the last `@`, and re-encoded with `encodeURIComponent` when rebuilding. **The unblock:** the founder then reported **GitHub has removed Supabase from their authorised applications too**, so there is now no route into the Supabase dashboard at all — the password cannot be reset there. So the Engine page grows a **"Try a different Postgres password"** box that appears ONLY after the database rejects credentials (never after a network failure — that would send them chasing the wrong problem): used for one run, never stored, never logged, never in the audit row, and still not an arbitrary-SQL hole because the only statements that can run remain the reviewed committed ones. +12 tests. Gates: api tsc + **310** vitest · admin build · doc-lint.
- **25 Jul (🔌 ENETUNREACH — the migration button was never going to work, and it wasn't the SQL):** the founder pressed Vida → Engine → Run it now and got `Failed: 20260725_client_inboxes — connect ENETUNREACH 2a05:d018:cb1:bb00:…:5432`. That address is **IPv6**. Supabase's direct database host (`db.<ref>.supabase.co`) has been **IPv6-only since Jan 2024** unless you pay for the IPv4 add-on, and **Railway containers have no IPv6 egress** — so the connection died before sending a byte. Nothing was wrong with the SQL, the password, or the endpoint: the address is simply unreachable from where we run, which means this button could never have worked and the whole "run migrations from Vida" workaround was resting on a connection that cannot exist. Supabase's own answer is the **pooler (Supavisor)**, which does publish IPv4: `postgres.<ref>@aws-0-<region>.pooler.supabase.com:5432` — port 5432 is SESSION mode, which behaves like a normal connection and runs DDL (6543 is transaction mode, wrong tool for migrations). The one thing not derivable is the **region**, and the founder can't look it up because the dashboard is behind the flagged GitHub account — so `lib/db-connection.ts` generates one candidate per Supabase region (SA/EU first) and the runner **probes for a reachable one before running any SQL** (so a migration is never half-applied across two different connections), then reports which host worked. Two safety details that matter: it **only** retries on routing errors (`ENETUNREACH`/`ENOTFOUND`/timeouts) and **stops dead on a credentials error**, because replaying a bad password against 18 regions is how an account gets locked out; and `safeHost()` means the response and the audit row carry host:port only, never the password. Vida's Engine now says *which* host it connected through plus a hint to set `DATABASE_URL` to the pooler string permanently, so the fallback stops being a fallback. +18 tests (`db-connection.test.ts`). Gates: api tsc + **298** vitest · admin build · doc-lint.

That is precisely the failure the audit rule exists to stop, and I did it in the same message where I said I had checked live. Both rows corrected in the inventory rather than left to mislead the next session. **The genuine gaps, now shipped —** #317: ① **`charge.dispute.closed` was unhandled**, so a dispute WE WON left the client permanently short (we keep the cash Stripe returns AND their wallet stays clawed back — us taking money for nothing) → restores on `won` only, idempotent on `refund_restore_<id>`, and note **`type:'adjustment'` is NOT in `credit_transactions`' CHECK constraint**: the first draft used it, which would have thrown, 500'd the webhook and had Stripe retry forever while the client was never restored (`manual_grant` is the valid type; pinned in a new `refund-policy.test.ts` so it can't come back); ② **the money stopped but the WORK didn't** — a clawed-back wallet blocks the next $4 approval, yet every enrollment already mid-sequence kept emailing prospects in a refunded client's name, burning our sending reputation for someone who had just taken their money back → active campaigns now pause on refund/dispute, reversibly, with an alert. #290: ③ **`error_events` may never have existed in prod** — its migration was written to be run by hand in the Supabase editor the founder has been locked out of since, and the handler swallows the insert failure by design, so capture may have been quietly degraded the whole time → the migration is now in `pending-migrations.ts`, runnable from **Vida → Engine → Run it now**; ④ **`uncaughtException` / `unhandledRejection` only `console.error`'d**, so the crash class — the most serious kind — was the *only* one that never alerted → both now record + alert through the same hourly throttle. Gates: api tsc + **280** vitest (+8) · doc-lint. **Founder action that makes #317 real: confirm Stripe → Webhooks is subscribed to `charge.refunded`, `charge.dispute.created` and `charge.dispute.closed`** — without those events, none of this code ever fires.

Also confirmed the flow chart's "optional Skip" on step 4 is a *description*, not a build item: refining the ICP is optional by default because the client's ICP is live from step 1, so the default path runs 1 → 2 → 3 → 5. - **25 Jul (🔎 PRE-MERGE AUDIT of the launch path — five defects, two of them shipping-broken):** founder: *"audit this before I merge."* Right call — the audit found five, run against the built code rather than from memory. **① Would have broken the entire cockpit:** `/operator/cockpit` selected `daily_send_limit` as a column, and **there is no such column** — the cap lives in `settings.daily_send_limit` JSONB (read by the send cron in `internal.ts` and the #511 auto-tune). A single bad column fails the whole PostgREST select, so Vida's cockpit would have read "Failed to load cockpit" for every client. **② Silent send-integrity failure — the worst class in this product:** the Auto-Pilot/Co-Pilot toggle wrote `copilot_mode` + `approve_before_send`. Both columns are real (migration `20260531_copilot_mode.sql`) but **nothing in the codebase reads either** — the actual gate is `settings.review_required` at `lib/figsy.ts:591`. The UI would have promised "every email waits for you on the Approvals tab" while the emails sent ungated. Fixed with a new `lib/campaign-settings.ts` (+13 tests) that owns where the gates really live: the toggle writes `settings.review_required` by **read-merge**, so `send_days` / `send_hour_utc` / `ab_subject_b…e` / reply-branching `steps` / `system_prompt` can never be clobbered by a settings write, and the two look-alike columns are kept in sync so they stop being a lie sitting in the table. New campaigns default to Co-Pilot even when the caller says nothing. **③ Crash on every render:** the "re-read people once the campaign is known" effect used `activeCampaign?.id` in its **dependency array** — evaluated during render, while the `const` was declared further down. `tsc` caught it; the Next build did not. **④ Misleading:** `GET /operator/asks` attributed every later client message to the last ask, so ordinary chat would display as "They said…" under our question — reading as if they'd answered when they hadn't; an answer is now only the turn immediately after the ask, and the 200-message window is stated in the response instead of pretended away. **⑤ Alert silent exactly when it matters:** `/operator/alerts` date-filtered the ICP read to 14 days, so "ICP approved, no campaign — they can't be worked" would never fire for a client onboarded more than two weeks ago and still not working; the filter is gone, and only the brand-new-client test uses the window. Also verified clean: all 31 UI→route URLs map · `icps.updated_at`, `figsy_enrollments.total_steps`, `leads.revealed_at`, `milla_sessions.title`, `milla_messages.client_id` all exist · `POST /icps/revise` route order + `requireAuth` correct · `requireMillaAccess` needs only a client record so a client CAN answer an ask · Co-Pilot's queue lands in `figsy_approval_queue`, which is what the Approvals tab reads. Gates: api tsc + **254** vitest (+13) · portal + admin build · doc-lint. Still no migration.
- **25 Jul (🚀 THE LAUNCH PATH — the shell was live, the features were not; built end to end, no SQL):** we walked the flow instead of building, and the founder named the real problem: *"the old portal, I used to create an ICP via a conversation with FIGSY… then generate people, assign them to a campaign, be suggested a campaign, edit a sequence, send a test email and hit go. Right now in Vida I can't see this process. The shell is here but the features are not."* Root cause is the same auth wall as the cockpit: every one of those endpoints is gated by `requireAuth` (a **client** Bearer JWT), and the admin app proxies with `x-admin-key` and **no client session** — so the operator could look at a campaign and never propose, fill, preview, test or run one. Built the operator twins plus the client's half (#520–#537), all on tables that already exist, so **none of it was blocked on the Supabase lockout**. **Vida** now runs the path end to end: **ICP by conversation** (`POST /operator/icp/chat`, seeded with the client's current active ICP so it refines rather than restarts; the field form stays as the precise-edit fallback) → **multi-select the people** (`GET /operator/people` — whole pool, masked emails stay masked, shows who is already enrolled) → **assign the picked ones** (`POST /operator/campaign/:id/assign`, `prepaid` so it never re-charges) → **Vida proposes the campaign, the operator approves** (`/campaign/suggest` → `/campaign/save`; deterministic when there's no LLM key — never invents, never fails the step) → **edit it** (name · the brief every email is written from · daily cap) with an **Auto-Pilot / Co-Pilot** choice (Co-Pilot writes `approve_before_send`; new campaigns start there on purpose) → **Vida drafts the sequence** against a real top-scoring lead then de-tokenises it into a template (`/sequence/suggest`) → **preview it as the prospect reads it** (`GET /operator/sequence/:id/preview`) → **email yourself a test** (`/campaign/:id/test`, subject-prefixed `[TEST · client]` — the last gate before a real prospect) → **Run it** → **see who's in it** and where each of them is (`/campaign/:id/enrollments`). Plus the **bell** (`GET /operator/alerts`, derived live from real rows — new client whose first ICP waits on us · ICP revised under a live campaign · replies to answer) and **"Ask them for these"**, which now genuinely reaches the client: `POST /operator/ask` writes into their own Milla thread, because the conversation IS the surface and no new table was needed. **Milla's half:** the thread **persists across visits** (it started blank every time, so an ask could never be seen, let alone answered), the client **revises their ICP by conversation** and it **goes live** — founder-locked, no gate on their own change — while alerting us because anyone already enrolled was picked against the OLD profile (`POST /icps/revise`), and they **see the sequence read-only** on My campaign. The top-left brand is now a link home in both consoles. **Two honesty fixes, both of which would have read as working:** assign counted successful `autoEnrollLead` calls, but that function returns `void` and bails silently on no verified email / suppression / a CRM match / exhausted FIGSY credits / an unconfigured sender — it would have reported "12 added" when 3 were, so the count now comes from `figsy_enrollments` and names what didn't make it; and because `autoEnrollLead` enrols into the client's most recent **active** campaign (it takes no campaign id), assigning to a paused campaign did nothing quietly — it now refuses with *"'X' isn't running — hit Run it first."* The `{{token}}` contract was extracted to `lib/sequence-tokens.ts` (+12 tests) so the suggest and preview halves can't drift into a literal `{{first_name}}` reaching a prospect. Gates: api tsc + **241** vitest (+12) · portal + admin build · doc-lint. **No migration.** Deferred by the founder: refining the approval gates.

Password reset is a dead end too — Supabase replied that the account is GitHub-linked and can only sign in that way. psql/Homebrew are not installed and the founder explicitly does not want new local tooling. So the migration now runs from **Vida**, using only what already exists: `lib/pending-migrations.ts` holds the reviewed, committed, **idempotent** statements as a string constant (NOT read from disk — `.sql` files are not copied into `dist/` by tsc, so a file read would pass locally and fail in prod), and `POST /operator/migrations/run` (admin-key gated, audited as `run_migration`) executes them against `DATABASE_URL` via node-postgres — the Supabase JS client speaks PostgREST and cannot do DDL. **The endpoint ignores the request body entirely**, so it can never become an arbitrary-SQL hole. - **24 Jul (🔎 OPUS VERIFY of the console PR — caught + fixed a money-for-nothing regression):** verify pass over the overnight Sonnet build (#1146). The nav/declutter work and the Vida escape fix hold up — links, API contract (`/figsy/campaigns` column names), the middleware rewrite (no fetch() breakage, no redirect loop, `/vida/*` correctly excluded) all check out. **But the PR shipped a real, money-losing regression.** Removing the client's self-serve FIGSY console took away the ONLY UI in the entire system that could create a campaign: `POST /figsy/campaigns` is client-JWT-only, the operator/admin side has **no** campaign-create endpoint at all, and nothing auto-creates one (the only other inserts are seed/demo + clone/apply-sequence, which need an existing campaign). Meanwhile `approveLead` charges the flat $4 and then calls `autoEnrollLead(..., {force:true})`, which **bails silently** on `if (!campaign) return` — and the call is `.catch(() => {})`. Net: a client pays $99, approves a lead, is **debited $4, gets no enrolment, no outreach, no error and no refund** — and no console could fix it. **Fixed three ways:** ① `approveLead` now fail-closes *before* the charge — no active campaign ⟹ status `no_campaign`, wallet untouched, claim released, founder alerted (`sends_stalled`); it never takes money for work it can't run (the same principle as the insufficient-funds gate). ② New operator endpoint `POST /operator/campaign/start` (admin-key gated, audited as `start_campaign`, idempotent, creates **ACTIVE** — the table default is `draft`, which would leave the client just as blocked) + a "Start campaign" button on the Vida per-client page that appears exactly when a client has no active campaign, explaining why approvals are blocked. ③ Both `/leads/:id/approve` and `/reveal` return a 409 `no_campaign`, and Milla translates the known error CODES into plain English (the portal's api helper surfaces `error`, not `message`, so a client would otherwise have literally read "no_campaign"). +2 regression tests (no charge, no reversal, claim released) — the test harness's table-agnostic mock was also made table-aware so the guard is actually exercised. Gates: api tsc + **229** vitest (+2) · portal + admin build clean · doc-lint green. No migration.

- **24 Jul (💰 MONEY MODEL REPLACED — ONE WALLET / work model, supersedes #492's 23-Jul hold re-time):** founder replaced the money model: **we do the work, we get paid.** A single dollar wallet per client · first purchase **$99** then free top-ups ($40/$100/$200) · the client's 👍 charges a flat **$4 per approved lead, FINAL** — no $1/$3 split shown, no hold, no capture-on-booking, no release, no TTL, no hand-backs · a **dead email is never charged** (the $4 is reversed in-flow) · **meetings are reported, not refunded** · only the client's 👍 moves money. **Built full-stack in one atomic switch:** website + legal terms (12 files, all "$1 reveal + $3 work" / auto-refund / two-wallet copy → $4-flat + $99 + one wallet); Milla portal (one wallet chip · $99 billing card · $4 approval card · reports spend = approved×$4 + cost-per-meeting · meetings report-only); Vida console (board columns/record/bookings/reports de-$3'd); API rails (`approve-lead.ts` → one atomic `try_charge_wallet($4)`; deleted the hold/capture/release/TTL machine from `credit-holds` callers, `figsy.ts`, `calendar.ts`, the stale-hold sweep; `stripe.ts` → one-wallet checkout `{amount_usd}` with a server $99-first-purchase gate + `increment_wallet` webhook + wallet claw-back; `/ledger`, `/milla-summary`, `/credits`, operator record → single dollar wallet). Migration `20260724_one_wallet.sql` (wallet column + backfill from old credits + `try_charge_wallet`/`increment_wallet` RPCs) — **owed on prod**. - **24 Jul (F1+F2 — Fable audit fixes):** F1 = made `chargeFigsyEnroll` tri-state (`charged`/`skipped`/`failed`) so a refund fires ONLY on a genuine charge — the #1139 double-$3 guard returned `true` on a hold-skip, so callers treating `true` as "credit taken" refunded on a later insert failure and inflated the wallet; all 3 call sites (autoEnrollLead's two insert-failure branches + the webhook/manual enroll loops) now refund only on `charged`, release the hold on `figsyHeld`, and take nothing on `skipped`/`failed`. F2 = corrected the stale BUILD-STATUS (now archived) `scripts/ship.sh` row (was 🟡 PR #1126 → ✅ 🟢, merged + in daily use). Gates: api tsc + 227 vitest · doc-lint green. No migration.
- **24 Jul (DOUBLE-$3 investigation — root-caused + guarded):** chased the flagged double-charge. **Confirmed real:** the $1 reveal is deduped per (client,email) by #424, but the **$3 work credit has NO per-lead dedup** — `holdFigsyCredit` is idempotent (its hold row) yet `chargeFigsyEnroll` never checked for an existing hold, so a held lead enrolled via any non-`figsyHeld` path (`autoEnrollLead` from icps/consent/intent/cron, or the manual `/campaigns/:id/enroll` + `/webhook/enrol` loops) charged the $3 twice. Primary managed path was already safe (`approve-lead` → `autoEnrollLead({figsyHeld:true})` skips the charge). **FIX:** `chargeFigsyEnroll` now returns early (no 2nd charge, enrollment still proceeds) when a `credit_holds` row (held/captured) already exists for (client,lead) — makes it idempotent w.r.t. the hold, closing the normal approve→enroll order for every enroll path. **STILL FLAGGED (founder ruling needed — money-model):** the REVERSE order — auto-enroll-on-source (`icps.ts:508`, gated behind `AUTO_OUTREACH_ENABLED`, **off today**) charges + works leads BEFORE the client approves, which both contradicts approve-then-reveal AND double-charges when the later approve holds (the hold doesn't exist yet at enroll time, so the guard can't catch it). Recommendation: in the managed model no lead should enroll/charge before the client's 👍 — gate the auto/self-serve enroll paths off for managed clients (or add a per-(client,lead) "work-charged-once" marker both paths honour). Safe today (flag off); must be resolved before `AUTO_OUTREACH_ENABLED` flips on at launch. Gates: api tsc + 227 vitest green.
- **24 Jul (DEEP BUG AUDIT — Vida + Milla consoles, 17 fixes):** ran 4 parallel fresh-eyes bug-hunters over the two consoles + the money/off-ramp + Nexus code, then verified each finding in-code and fixed the real ones (Opus, to save Fable usage before handoff). **Money-integrity (highest stakes):** ① stale-hold sweep no longer refunds the $3 on a co-pilot enrollment paused at the Send gate (`next_send_at=null` was mis-read as dead — now matches on status alone); ② an opt_out/unsubscribe reply now RELEASES the held $3 immediately (was trapped 60 days until the sweep); ③ a booking-row insert failure still CAPTURES the $3 (real meeting was leaking revenue); ④ the calendar retry ladder is now idempotent (deterministic Google event id → a retry can't create a duplicate invite); ⑤ concurrent double-booking blocked by a partial unique index + 409; ⑥ the sourcing-cost preview now subtracts blocklist + suppression (was under-stating OUR PDL cost). **Client-facing money (Milla):** the Reports "Spend" was summing credit COUNTS as $1 (a $3 work-credit read as $1, released holds counted forever) → now true dollars (reveals×$1 + bookings×$3) from real server-side all-time counts (was reading a capped 50-row ledger / 4-row replies rail); dashboard balance now shows BOTH pools (reveal + work) so "need $4 to approve" is legible. **Nexus:** the persona query is now an explicit `client_id` filter (fence = filter, not assumption); rates clamped to ≤100%; null-guards. **Vida UI:** `parseSourceIntent` no longer hijacks normal commands ("find the CEO"); rebook datetime blocks past times; null-guards on the Nexus panel. **Dismissed 1 false positive** (the Nexus Enable button intentionally lets you turn the switch on before a client is confident). **Flagged, not fixed** (pre-existing, needs flow review): a possible double-$3 on the manual-enroll-after-approve path — the PRIMARY approve→enroll path is SAFE (`figsyHeld` guard skips `chargeFigsyEnroll`). and the design references committed to `docs/mv-previews/`. - **22 Jul (cont.²) — 📅 BLOCK 1.4 SHIPPED: calendar booking live in code (#361b + #368) — FIGSY books meetings straight into the client's Google Calendar via a prospect self-serve page.** The plumbing existed but was dead (googleapis stripped → every `/calendar/*` route 500'd; slots/book auth-gated so a cold prospect couldn't book; OAuth `state` unsigned). Built (Opus): googleapis re-added (lazy-load kept) · **#368** HMAC-signed OAuth state (10-min TTL, timingSafeEqual) · per-lead 90-day signed **booking token** = the auth for two public rate-limited routes (`/calendar/public/:token/slots|book`) · shared `performBooking` (freebusy race re-check → real Google event + Meet link → `calendar_bookings` → `meeting_booked` outcome → campaign KPI) with one-active-booking-per-lead 409 dedup · public portal page **`/book/<token>`** (prospect's own timezone, honest error states) · FIGSY now drops the per-lead tokenised link into sequences when the calendar is connected (falls back to static `booking_url`) · slots drawn in the CALENDAR's timezone not server UTC · Google auth failure flips `calendar_booking_enabled` off honestly. **Fable verify caught 3 pre-merge: (F1)** the booking page used React-19 `use(params)` on React 18 → would crash for every prospect — fixed to Next-14 params; **(F2)** token-type confusion — an emailed booking token also passed OAuth-state verification, reopening #368 via side door → type claims added + cross-replay tests; **(F3)** public book accepted ANY start/end (a prospect could create a 10-hour 3am event) → requested slot must now match a server-offered slot. Gates: api tsc + 185/185 · portal tsc + build. Migration `20260722_calendar_booking_live.sql` (idempotent). #361 #368 → 🩷. **Walk needs 🧍: run the migration + `railway up` api/portal + set `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`/`GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` on @kind/api (Google Cloud OAuth consent + web credentials) — feature deploys safely dormant until set.** Board 🟢90·🩷148·🟣1·🟡18·🔴218·⏸5·Σ480, lint green. _Code (api+portal) + docs._
